Well, um... maybe just a little bit? I mean, offices are naturally kind of noisy, right? But I guess... yeah, I have been a bit jumpy. 

Like, if the printer jams loudly, or if a heavy door slams down the hall, or even if one of my coworkers just walks up behind my desk to ask a question... I just... I jump. Like, I jump out of my skin, like something terrible is happening all over again. And then it takes me several minutes to actually settle down and get my heart rate back to normal. 

I try really hard to hide it, though, because I don't want anyone at work to think I'm being dramatic or, you know, fragile. I usually just laugh it off so they don't worry or think I'm acting weird. That's a normal reaction, right? Being a little jumpy like that after an accident?

Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's because I'm looking for danger or anything like that. I know I'm safe in my own apartment. But, um... I guess I haven't been sleeping great.

I'll suddenly get hit with this really sharp image of the worker on the floor, and all the soot in the air. And it just feels like my body reacts before I can even stop it. Like my heart jumps and I'm right back there in the warehouse. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too dramatic... is it normal to be reacting like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding anything that reminds you of the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, I promise I'm still getting things done. But... It's just... even when I go to open an email about those types of policies, my stomach just completely drops. I know I need to just push through it, and I will, I just... needed a little break from it, I guess. I really thought I'd just bounce back after a day or two, you know? Like, it's just a week, so maybe I'm overreacting by coming in so soon... Do you think I should have just waited a bit longer to see if it would go away on its own?\n\nDoctor: Are there any people, places, or activities that you're avoiding because they remind you of the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, besides what I mentioned before about the work files and the phone calls, I don't think so. \n\nI'm still going to the grocery store and, you know, seeing my friends when they invite me out. I really don't want to be a burden on anyone or ruin their plans, so I just... I make sure I still go out and do normal things with them. I'm trying really hard to keep my regular routine going outside of the office, I promise. \n\nDoes that... I mean, is that a good sign? That I'm not just avoiding everything?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having difficulty concentrating since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Just focusing on moving forward?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble falling asleep or staying asleep due to being constantly on the lookout for potential threats or dangers?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's because I'm looking for danger or anything like that. I know I'm safe in my own apartment. But, um... I guess I haven't been sleeping great. \n\nActually, that's kind of the main reason I decided to come in. I've barely been sleeping at all since the inspection. I wake up all sweaty, just completely wide awake, feeling like I'm right back there near the blast. \n\nIt's probably just my body still working through the adrenaline, right? I'm sure it'll pass once I get back into my normal routine. I just thought maybe there was something mild I could take to help me get a few good nights of rest so I can be sharp for work. Um, I think we just talked about that, right? The hobbies and things? Like I said, I'm really trying to keep up with everything.

People? Oh, no, definitely not. I'd never avoid my coworkers or anything like that, that would be so unprofessional. I really pride myself on being friendly and a good team player. 

But, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest... I have been sort of avoiding some of the work itself? Like, I've been avoiding the actual warehouse file. And... well, I've been letting calls about any of our industrial accounts just go to voicemail lately. 

It's probably silly, I know, but even just the thought of opening those specific emails makes my stomach drop. I'm still keeping up with all my other underwriting work, I promise! I'm really trying to stay on top of my normal caseload. I just... I need a little space from those specific types of accounts right now. That makes sense, doesn't it? To just ease back into the harder stuff so I don't get overwhelmed?

I might read an email twice instead of once, but I'm still getting everything done on time. I really don't want my boss to think I can't handle my job, so I'm pushing myself to stay on top of things. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble concentrating. I'm doing okay with that, right? That's what I should be doing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling constantly on edge or easily startled since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Well, um... maybe just a little bit? I mean, offices are naturally kind of noisy, right? But I guess... yeah, I have been a bit jumpy. \n\nLike, if the printer jams loudly, or if a heavy door slams down the hall, or even if one of my coworkers just walks up behind my desk to ask a question... I just... I jump. Like, I jump out of my skin, like something terrible is happening all over again. And then it takes me several minutes to actually settle down and get my heart rate back to normal. \n\nI try really hard to hide it, though, because I don't want anyone at work to think I'm being dramatic or, you know, fragile. I usually just laugh it off so they don't worry or think I'm acting weird. That's a normal reaction, right? Being a little jumpy like that after an accident?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for what happened during the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, it was just an accident, right? The scaffolding just... it just gave way. \n\nI know I was the one who insisted we go down that specific aisle to check the inventory tags, but... I was just doing my job, you know? I was just following the protocol for the inspection. I try not to dwell on the \"what ifs,\" because I know that's not helpful. I don't want to make it all about me when other people were there too. \n\nSo, no, I don't think I feel guilty. I'm just trying to move past it and focus on my work. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? Just focusing on moving forward?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble falling asleep or staying asleep due to being constantly on the lookout for potential threats or dangers?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's because I'm looking for danger or anything like that. I know I'm safe in my own apartment. But, um... I guess I haven't been sleeping great. \n\nActually, that's kind of the main reason I decided to come in. I've barely been sleeping at all since the inspection. I wake up all sweaty, just completely wide awake, feeling like I'm right back there near the blast. \n\nIt's probably just my body still working through the adrenaline, right? I'm sure it'll pass once I get back into my normal routine. I just thought maybe there was something mild I could take to help me get a few good nights of rest so I can be sharp for work. It's just normal work stress, probably. \n\nI really do try to unwind, though. I don't want to be one of those people who just stews in anxiety all day. I'm doing my best to stay positive and calm. That's the best way to handle it, right? Just trying to keep a good attitude?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? Just focusing on moving forward?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble falling asleep or staying asleep due to being constantly on the lookout for potential threats or dangers?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I really don't want to be a burden on anyone or make it seem like I'm slacking off. I'm doing my best to just power through the rest of it, you know? I think I'm doing okay with the other stuff, don't you? As long as I'm getting most of my work done, it's not too bad, right?
